Well, um... I guess "impulses" sounds a little strong, but... I suppose I have made a few quick decisions lately. I just really wanted everything to be perfect for the showcase, you know?

Um, interrupting? I wouldn't say I do it on purpose, or to be rude. I really try to be a good listener, I promise. 

It's just... well, lately, especially when I start explaining the platform to someone, I just get so... engaged, you know? Like, I'll start talking about the architecture, and then that leads right into the funding strategy, and the cultural impact it's going to have, and the deployment plan... and it just all connects so perfectly in my head. 

Sometimes people have mentioned that they can't get a word in edge-wise, which makes me feel terrible, really. I don't mean to talk over anyone. It's just that the thoughts are arriving so fast, and they're all so important to the big picture, that if I pause, I'm afraid I'll lose the thread. It just feels like the ideas are moving faster than I can even speak them. 

That happens to everyone when they're really passionate about a big project, right? I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful of letting other people talk, though. I hope I'm not coming across as too overbearing.

Um, difficulty completing tasks? Well, I wouldn't say I'm failing at my job or anything. I'm actually getting a lot of incredible ideas down. 

I guess... well, this is actually kind of why I agreed to come in. It's just that lately, keeping my mind on one specific thing has been a little tricky. It's a creative process, really. It's not that I'm just easily distracted in a bad way... it's more like I'm highly responsive to inspiration, right? I mean, a lot of successful developers work that way, don't they? I'm sure it'll all come together before the deadline. I really am trying my best to stay on track.

They... they actually put me on a temporary leave. Just to rest, they said. I hope that doesn't sound too bad? We've had a few more arguments than usual, mostly because I've been buying a few things for the project that she thinks we don't need. But it's just an investment, really. Do you think it's normal for partners to get a little worried when you're taking a big leap like this? I just want to make sure I'm not making things too difficult for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulses more often than usual over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... It's just that lately, keeping my mind on one specific thing has been a little tricky. Like, I'll be in the middle of fixing a rendering bug, which I'm usually very good at, I promise. But then, you know, a Slack ping will pop up, or I'll notice a color pattern on my screen, or even just hear a siren outside... and suddenly I'm pulled into this whole new, brilliant idea for the platform. \n\nAnd I just have to follow that new idea, you know? So sometimes I might not finish that exact line of code right that second. But that's just because I'm seeing so many connections everywhere. It's a creative process, really. Um, significant problems? Well... I guess I sort of touched on this just a second ago, but I really wouldn't call them *problems*, exactly. More like... temporary adjustments, maybe? I really am trying to manage everything as best as I can, I promise.

But, um, I suppose the focus thing did play a part in... well, the leave from work. Like I mentioned, I just got so caught up in how brilliant the new deployment was going to be that I kind of... skipped the approval step and pushed the changes live myself. I just wanted to help the company, you know? But my employer felt it was best I take a break. I'm sure they just want me to rest, right? They know how hard I work.

And, well, at home... I guess the distractions and all the new ideas have caused a bit of a ripple. My wife has said things feel really unstable right now. We've been arguing more, mostly about the money I've been spending to get this project off the ground. I feel awful that she's stressed about it. I really just want to provide a great future for us. It's just a rough patch that we'll get through once the project takes off, don't you think? I'm really trying to be a good husband, I am.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, relationships changing... well, I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record, and I'm really sorry if I'm not giving you the exact answers you're looking for. I just want to make sure I'm being helpful. 

But, um, I guess I'd just point back to what I was saying before about work and home. I mean, my employer putting me on leave... that definitely changed my relationship with my boss and my team, at least for now. I really just wanted to show them initiative by deploying those changes myself, you know? I didn't want to cause any friction. I'm sure they'll see that once things settle down, right? 

And, um, with my wife... yeah, things have changed a bit there, too. Like I said, she feels like things are really unstable right now. The arguments we've been having, and her being upset about the money I've spent on the project... it's definitely put a strain on us. I feel so guilty about it, I really do. I'm trying so hard to be the partner she needs and to make sure she knows I'm doing all this for our future. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the whole picture here.

I can sit still when I need to, I promise. That's... that's a normal thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or blurting out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Um, interrupting? Like, I'll start talking about the architecture, and then that leads right into the funding strategy, and the cultural impact it's going to have, and the deployment plan... and it just all connects so perfectly in my head. \n\nSometimes people have mentioned that they can't get a word in edge-wise, which makes me feel terrible, really. I don't mean to talk over anyone. It's just that the thoughts are arriving so fast, and they're all so important to the big picture, that if I pause, I'm afraid I'll lose the thread. It just feels like the ideas are moving faster than I can even speak them. \n\nThat happens to everyone when they're really passionate about a big project, right? I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful of letting other people talk, though. I really am trying my best to stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to cut back on any social or work activities because of these difficulties with focus and distraction?\n\nPatient: Um, cut back? I—I usually have a really great attention span, I promise. I'm usually very reliable with my focus at work. \n\nIt's really just been... maybe the last three months or so? Um, it started right around the time my platform was selected for the Glendale showcase. Which is this really huge, wonderful opportunity, so I've just been a bit excited, I think. \n\nIs it... I mean, is it pretty normal to get a little scattered when you have a big project like that going on? I hope I'm making sense, I just want to make sure I'm not overreacting to normal stress.\n\nDoctor: Are these distractions causing you a lot of distress or are they significantly interfering with your work or personal life?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it has caused a little bit of a bump at work. I mean, I was just trying to be proactive, you know? I had this really great idea for the showcase, and I just... I deployed some changes on my own without waiting for the usual approvals. I thought I was helping speed things up, but my employer felt differently. They... they actually put me on a temporary leave. Just to rest, they said. I hope that doesn't sound too bad? I really am a good employee, I just got a little ahead of myself. \n\nAnd at home... well, my wife has been a little stressed, I think. She says things feel a bit unstable right now. We've had a few more arguments than usual, mostly because I've been buying a few things for the project that she thinks we don't need. But it's just an investment, really. Do you think it's normal for partners to get a little worried when you're taking a big leap like this? I just want to make sure I'm not making things too difficult for her.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulses more often than usual over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess \"impulses\" sounds a little strong, but... I suppose I have made a few quick decisions lately. I just really wanted everything to be perfect for the showcase, you know? \n\nLike with the update I mentioned... I pushed it through without the usual approval because, well, waiting for another whole review cycle just felt like I'd be letting this amazing opportunity die. I was just trying to be efficient, I promise. \n\nAnd, um, regarding the things my wife was worried about... I did spend some money we hadn't exactly agreed on beforehand. Just on things I knew we'd need, like some extra GPUs, some print materials, and a few promo videos. I thought of it as an investment in our future, really. But I can see now why it might have surprised her. \n\nIs it... I mean, it's pretty common to get a little overeager when you're passionate about your work, right? I'm really trying to be more mindful of it now. I hope I'm not sounding too irresponsible.\n\nDoctor: Have your quick decisions or actions caused problems in your relationships with friends or family beyond what you've mentioned with your wife?\n\nPatient: No, no, I don't think so. I mean, I really try to be a good friend and a good brother. I haven't been fighting with anyone else, I promise. \n\nI guess... well, maybe I haven't been seeing my friends quite as much lately? But that's just because I've been so focused on the showcase, you know? They're all really supportive, I think. I haven't done anything to upset them. At least, I really hope I haven't. It's okay to just be a little busy with work for a while, right? I'm sure they understand.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical restlessness or fidgeting, like tapping your hands or feet, especially when you're supposed to be sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, physical restlessness? I... I don't think so. I mean, I sit at a desk all day for work, so I'm pretty used to being still. \n\nI guess sometimes I might bounce my leg a little bit, but that's just normal energy, isn't it? Like, when you're really excited about a project and you just want to get to work on it? I'm sure everyone does that a little bit. I definitely wouldn't say I'm fidgety in a bad way. I can sit still when I need to, I promise. That's... that's a normal thing, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or blurting out answers before they finish speaking?\n\nPatient: Um, interrupting? I wouldn't say I do it on purpose, or to be rude. I really try to be a good listener, I promise. \n\nIt's just... well, lately, especially when I start explaining the platform to someone, I just get so... engaged, you know? Like, I'll start talking about the architecture, and then that leads right into the funding strategy, and the cultural impact it's going to have, and the deployment plan... and it just all connects so perfectly in my head. \n\nSometimes people have mentioned that they can't get a word in edge-wise, which makes me feel terrible, really. I don't mean to talk over anyone. It's just that the thoughts are arriving so fast, and they're all so important to the big picture, that if I pause, I'm afraid I'll lose the thread. It just feels like the ideas are moving faster than I can even speak them. \n\nThat happens to everyone when they're really passionate about a big project, right? I'm definitely going to try to be more mindful of letting other people talk, though. I'm sure it'll all come together before the deadline.
